<!-- page 1 -->
NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS
Lee Jack Sr. • Jesse Thompson • Jason E. Whiting • Steve Williams • Dawnafe
Whitesinger
"We are Navajo County" 
NOTICE OF PUBLIC HEARING AND AGENDA
NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS
Tuesday, April 11, 2017
ONE OR MORE SUPERVISORS MAY ATTEND VIA VIDEO AND/OR
TELEPHONICALLY 
The Navajo County Board of Supervisors will hold a meeting open to the public on Tuesday, April 11, 2017, in the
Supervisors’ Chamber in the Navajo County Governmental Complex, Holbrook, Arizona. The Board may vote to
meet in a closed executive session to discuss certain matters as indicated on the following agenda. The Board
may meet in executive session pursuant to A.R.S. § 38-431.03(A) (3) for legal advice on any item.
Persons with disabilities who need accommodation to attend or participate in the meeting may contact the Clerk of the
Board’s office at (928) 524-4053; at least 48 hours prior to the meeting so an accommodation can be arranged.
Items on the agenda may be considered out of order at the Chairman’s discretion. A copy of the agenda background
material provided to the Board, with the exception of items to be considered in executive session, is available for public
inspection at Navajo County website, www.navajocountyaz.gov 
9:00 a.m. Call To Order: Pledge of Allegiance and Invocation
           
1. CALL TO THE PUBLIC: Individuals may address the Board on any relevant issue for
up to 5 minutes. At the close of the call to the public, Board members may not respond
to any comments but may respond to criticism, ask staff to review a matter or ask that a
matter be placed on a future agenda.
 
 
2. CONSENT AGENDA: These items are considered to be routine or administrative in
nature and will be approved in a single motion. A Board member may request for any
item to be removed from the consent agenda and considered on the regular agenda:
 
 
a. Minutes: March 28, 2017.  
 
b. Warrant & Voucher Report over $1000 March 2017.  
 
c. Constable Reports: Winslow Precinct #2, March; Show Low Precinct #5, March; and
Pinetop-Lakeside Precinct #6, March 2017.
 
 
d. Five (5) year financing arrangement with KS State Bank to purchase 33 desktops, 27
laptops, 27 docking stations, 8 MDT docking stations, 10 monitors and 68 keyboards. 
 
 
e. Appointment of Candace Annette Kelley, Arthur Joe Kelley, Jr., and Carol Sletten as
Democratic Precinct Committeemen for their respective precincts.
 
 
f. Appointment of Travis Thorne and Shawn T. Redd as Republican Precinct
Committeemen for their respective precincts.

<!-- page 2 -->
g. 2017 Fair Housing Proclamation.  
 
h. Cooperative Law enforcement Agreement with the USDA, Forest Service
Apache-Sitgreaves National Forests for $10,000 for cooperative law enforcement efforts.
 
 
i. Intergovernmental Agreement for Law Enforcement Services between the Coconino
County Sheriff's Office and Navajo County Sheriff's Office for law enforcement
assistance in southeastern Coconino County in the Forest Lakes District. 
 
 
3. PUSH PROGRAM:  
 
a. Presentation to April Wellness Warrior, Aleen Hamilton.  
Presented By: Callie Jensen
 
4. BOARD OF SUPERVISORS:  
 
a. Presentation to Kenadi Stuart, Mylee Demuth and Damon Attakai for their 2017 Elk's
Hoop Shoot State and Regional Championships.
 
Presented By: Supervisor Jason Whiting
 
b. Presentation regarding the 4th Annual Holbrook Route 66 Festival.  
Presented By: City of Holbrook, Kathleen Smith
 
c. Consideration of a Proclamation declaring the month of April 2017 "Child Abuse
Prevention & Awareness Month".
 
Presented By: C.J. Wischmann
 
5. ADMINISTRATIVE SERVICES:  
 
a. Consideration and possible approval of Personnel Actions.
Presented By: Eric Scott
 
 
b. County Financial Update.  
Presented By: Bryan Layton

<!-- page 3 -->
6. SHERIFF'S OFFICE:  
 
a. Consideration of a proclamation declaring April 9 - April 15, 2017 as "National
Telecommunicator's Week".
 
Presented By: Sheriff K.C. Clark
 
b. Consideration of Agreement 2017-021 with the Department of Public Safety for the
transfer of one Virtual Training Simulator.
Presented By: Sheriff K.C. Clark 
 
 
7. ELECTIONS:  
 
a. Consideration of Revocation of authority of Porter Creek Domestic Water
Improvement District (DWID).
 
Presented By: Rayleen Richards
 
b. Consideration of call for an election to fill the remaining terms of three (3) vacant
governing board positions on the Porter Creek Domestic Water Improvement
District on Aug. 29, 2017.
 
Presented By: Rayleen Richards
 
8. INFORMATION TECHNOLOGY:  
 
a. Consideration of the Microsoft Enterprise Agreement starting April 1, 2017 for the
next three years. 
 
Presented By: Ken Dewitt
 
9. WHITE MOUNTAIN LAKES COUNTY RECREATION IMPROVEMENT
DISTRICT: Board of Directors Session:
 
 
a. Consideration and approval to hire Phil Stratton Electric and Perkins Cinders to fix a
broken culvert and electrical repair at 2015 Silver Creek Drive. 
Presented By: Melissa Buckley
 
 
10. COUNTY BUSINESS UPDATE: Report from Board members, County Attorney and
Assistant County Manager.

<!-- page 4 -->
11. ADJOURN  
 
Dated: April 7, 2017 NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS 
Posted: 11:45 a.m. By:MB                         
Melissa W. Buckley - Clerk of the Board

Board of Supervisors Regular 2.d.        
Meeting Date: 04/11/2017  
Title: Kansas State Bank Financing Agreement for NCSO Computers
Submitted For: Paige Peterson, Interim Finance Director 
Submitted By: Paige Peterson, Interim Finance Director
Department: Administrative Services Division: Finance
Motion before the Board:
Five (5) year financing arrangement with KS State Bank to purchase 33 desktops, 27 laptops, 27
docking stations, 8 MDT docking stations, 10 monitors and 68 keyboards. 
Background:
The Sheriff's Office is in need of new computers.  Financing through KS State Bank helps with
cash flow and enables the Jail Enhancement Fund to be able to afford the quarterly payments so
no general fund money is required. 
Fiscal Impact
Fiscal Year: 2017
Budgeted Y/N: Y
Amount Requested: 5,168 
Fiscal Impact:
Five year financing arrangement.  Down payment of $5,168 is due at time of closing (April 30, 2017
or prior).  Four payments of $5,168 are due each year until January 2020.  Down payment and
quarterly payments will be paid from Jail Enhancement funds.  Amortization schedule is attached. 
Attachments
KS State Bank Contract 
Form Review
Inbox Reviewed By Date
Paige Peterson Melissa Buckley 04/06/2017 12:26 PM
Form Started By: Paige Peterson Started On: 03/29/2017 03:24 PM
Final Approval Date: 04/06/2017

<!-- page 426 -->
Board of Supervisors Regular 2.e.        
Meeting Date: 04/11/2017  
Title: Appointment of Democratic Committeemen
Submitted By: Donna Hurstrom, Deputy Clerk of the Board
Department: Board of Supervisors
Motion before the Board:

Board of Supervisors Regular 2.g.        
Meeting Date: 04/11/2017  
Title: Fair Housing Proclamation
Submitted By: Melissa Buckley, Clerk of the Board of Supervisors
Department: Administrative Services Division: Finance
Motion before the Board:
2017 Fair Housing Proclamation. 
Background:
The Civil Rights Act of 1968 and Fair Housing Amendments Act of 1988 declare a national
policy prohibiting discrimination in the sale, rental, leasing, and financing of housing or land to be
used in the construction of housing or in the provision of brokerage services on the basis of race,
color, religion, sex, disability, familial status, or national origin. The Housing and Community
Development Act of 1974 as amended requires that all applicants for Community Development
Block Grant funds certify that they shall affirmatively further fair housing. Navajo County’s
proclamation and resolution declaring April 2017 Fair Housing Month for Navajo County and the
dissemination thereof will reaffirm the County’s commitment to furthering fair housing. 
Attachments
2017 Fair Housing Proclamation 
Form Review
Form Started By: Melissa Buckley Started On: 04/06/2017 01:04 PM
Final Approval Date: 04/06/2017

<!-- page 429 -->
FAIR HOUSING PROCLAMATION 
 
 
 
HEREAS, The National Fair Housing Law of 1986, as amended by the Fair 
Housing Amendments Act of 1988 prohibits discrimination in housing and 
declares it a national policy to provide within constitutional limits, for fair 
housing in the United States; and 
 
 
HEREAS, the principle of Fair Housing is not only national law and national 
policy but a fundamental human concept and entitlement for all Americans; 
and 
 
 
HEREAS, April has traditionally been designated as Fair Housin g Month in 
the United States; 
 
 
 
OW, THEREFORE, the Navajo County Board of Supervisors, do hereby 
proclaim April as Fair Housing Month in Navajo County and do hereby urge all  
Citizens of this community to comply with the letter and spirit of the Fair 
Housing Law. 
 
 
PASSED, ADOPTED AND APPROVED by the Board of Supervisors on April 11, 2017. 
 
 
_____________________________ 
Jesse Thompson, Chairman

Board of Supervisors Regular 3.a.        
Meeting Date: 04/11/2017  
Title: Wellness Warrior April
Submitted By: Melissa Buckley, Clerk of the Board of Supervisors
Department: PUSH Program
Presented By: Callie Jensen
Subject:
Presentation to April Wellness Warrior, Aleen Hamilton.
Background: 
Please tell me why you think you were nominated? Well, to tell you the truth, I don't know
other than maybe because everyone always sees me walking/almost running around the
complex and because every morning I am in the gym before work.
What health changes have you incorporated into your life recently? I have always been
very active.  I really enjoy working out in the mornings.  I try to eat right and drink a lot of
water. 
What benefits have you seen from making some changes? I just feel better when I work out,
and it keeps me young.  I have a bad back from a skiing accident when I was young, so I
keep moving and stretching to keep the pain away.  And I like to eat chocolate while sitting
at my desk, so working out keeps the pounds off. 
What have you accomplished in the past year? What I try to do is just keep going.  I don't
work out for weight loss, I just like to maintain where I'm at, and it keeps me healthy.  I
don't really set goals, but every day I wake up, I may say, well, maybe five more minutes
today.
How has the PUSH Program helped? The PUSH Program helps me, because I really don't
like to drink water, really I don't like to drink anything, but I know I have to drink to keep
healthy so I do it!!

Navajo County               
Proclamation 
 
 
NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS PROCLAMATION 
April 2017 “Child Abuse Prevention & Awareness Month” 
 
WHEREAS, Buena Vista Children’s Services, Inc. is a community -based, grassroots, nonprofit 
organization that offers child abuse prevention awareness, ch ildren’s advocacy and child care 
recruitment services for the Department of Economic Security, Education & Professional 
Development, in Navajo, Apache and Yavapai counties; and 
 
WHEREAS, every child is entitled to be loved, cared for, secure and safe, and it is the 
responsibility of our society to protect every child’s inalienable right to life, liberty and the pursuit 
of happiness; and 
 
WHEREAS, the hidden epidemic of child abuse, which crosses all boundaries of income, race, 
religion and ethnicity, continues to be one of our most serious public health issues, with over 90 
percent of abuse perpetrated by people that children know, love and trust; and 
 
WHEREAS, children who survive abuse endure lasting physical, mental, and emotional scars, 
including feelings of shame, self -doubt, poor self -image, the inability to trust others, learning 
disabilities, sleep disturbances, dating violence, difficulty in trust and coping skills, and eating, 
speech or post-traumatic stress disorders; and 
 
WHEREAS, child abuse and neglect can be reduced by providing safe havens and emotional, 
social, legal and financial support for victims and at -risk families; and by holding offenders 
accountable, we can ensure that all of Arizona’s children grow to their full potential as healthy 
citizens; and 
 
WHEREAS, providing the proper support and enhancing a child’s understanding of his or her 
own self-worth are crucial components in providing a bright future and raising healthy Arizona 
children. 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, by the Board of Supervisors, on behalf of Navajo County, do her eby 
proclaim the month of April 2017, as “Child Abuse Prevention & Awareness Month” in Navajo 
County and urge all citizens to cherish our children and protect them from harm. 
 
PASSED, ADOPTED AND APPROVED by the Board of Supervisors on April 11, 2017. 
 
 
____________________________ 
Jesse Thompson, Chairman

_______________________________ 
Melissa W. Buckley, Clerk of the Board

National Telecommunicator's Week  
April 9-15, 2017 
Whereas emergencies can occur at any time that require police, fire or emergency medical services; and 
Whereas when an emergency occurs the prompt response of police officers, firefighters and paramedics is  
critical to the protection of life and preservation of property; and 
Whereas the safety of our police officers and firefighters is dependent upon the quality and accuracy of 
information obtained from citizens who telephone the Navajo County Sheriff’s police-fire communications 
center; and 
Whereas Public Safety Dispatchers are the first and most critical contact our citizens have with emergency 
services; and 
Whereas Public Safety Dispatchers are the single vital link for our police officers and firefighters by monitoring 
their activities by radio, providing them information and insuring their safety; and 
Whereas Public Safety Dispatchers of the Navajo County Sheriff’s Office have contributed substantially to the 
apprehension of criminals, suppression of fires and treatment of patients; and 
Whereas each dispatcher has exhibited compassion, understanding and professionalism during the 
performance of their job in the past year.  
Now, Therefore Be It Proclaimed, by the Board of Supervisors of Navajo County and the Navajo County Sheriff 
that the week of April 9th through April 15th, 2017 is designated to be National Telecommunicator's Week in 
Navajo County, Arizona in honor of the men and women whose diligence and professionalism keep our city 
and citizens safe. 
Passed, adopted and approved by the Board of Supervisors on April 11, 2017.

Board of Supervisors Regular 7.a.        
Meeting Date: 04/11/2017  
Title: Porter Creek Revote Authority 4/11/17
Submitted By: Rayleen Richards, Interim Elections Director
Department: Elections
Presented By: Rayleen Richards
Motion before the Board:
Consideration of Revocation of authority of Porter Creek Domestic Water Improvement
District (DWID).
Background:
The Porter Creek DWID currently has only two (2) duly appointed or elected governing board
member. Hence the district can no longer function as they necessarily also lack a quorum. The
Board of Supervisors is empowered to revoke the authority of the district governing board in order
to rectify the lack of a quorum. In order to return the district to functionality, the Board of
Supervisors may, after revocation of authority, subsequently call for an election to fill the
vacancies on the governing board or the Board of Supervisors may assume the duties of the
district’s governing board permanently.
 
48-1012. Elected board of directors; initial members; qualifications; terms; filling vacancy;
chairperson; qualifications of electors
C. If a vacancy in the district board occurs due to death or disability or any other cause other than
resignation, the board of directors of the district shall appoint a qualified elector of the district to
fill the office for the remaining portion of that term. If there is a vacancy in the district board due
to resignation, the district board shall accept the resignation and appoint a qualified elector to fill
the remaining portion of that term of office. If the district board lacks a quorum for any reason for
more than thirty days, the county board of supervisors may revoke the authority of the appointed
or elected board of directors pursuant to section 48-1016.
 
48-1016. Revocation of authority of elected board of directors
The board of supervisors of the county in which a domestic water improvement district or
domestic wastewater improvement district is located may at any time revoke the authority of an
elected board of directors in order to protect the residents of the district. Any district in which the
authority of an elected board of directors is revoked pursuant to this section shall be governed by
the board of supervisors as provided by section 48-908 and the board of supervisors shall have the
option of calling for new elections for the district board of directors.
 
Recommendation: 
Request the authority of the Porter Creek DWID be revoked pursuant to A.R.S. §§

<!-- page 468 -->
Request the authority of the Porter Creek DWID be revoked pursuant to A.R.S. §§
48-1012 & 48-1016.

Board of Supervisors Regular 7.b.        
Meeting Date: 04/11/2017  
Title: Porter Creek DWID COE 4/11/17
Submitted By: Rayleen Richards, Interim Elections Director
Department: Elections
Presented By: Rayleen Richards
Motion before the Board:
Consideration of call for an election to fill the remaining terms of three (3) vacant governing
board positions on the Porter Creek Domestic Water Improvement District on Aug. 29,
2017.
Background:
The Board of Supervisors, acting as the Porter Creek DWID governing board, needs to call an
election to fill vacancies on the Porter Creek DWID governing board and allow the district to
return to self-governance. Two (2) positions whose terms expire in November of 2020 and one (1)
position whose term expires in November of 2018 are available. Pursuant to A.R.S. § 16-226, the
call for an election need to be made at least 120 days prior to the election date. 120 days prior to
the Aug. 29, 2017 election date is May 1, 2017.
 
16-226. Nonpartisan elections; time of calling; definition
A. Nonpartisan elections shall be called no later than one hundred twenty days before the date of
holding the election.
B. For purposes of this chapter, "nonpartisan" means an election that is held by a special district
established pursuant to title 48 and that is not held concurrently with the general election.
 
Recommendation: 
Request an election be called to fill the remaining terms of three (3) vacant governing
board positions for the Porter Creek DWID. The election will take place Aug. 29, 2017, be
an all mail and will be conducted by Navajo County Elections Division.

Navajo County uses Microsoft products to run major systems to support the organization.  This
not only includes Office products and email which are used by practically every employee but the
products in the back room that supports every database and application used by the County.  A
few years ago the County decided to switch from a traditional hard phone - PBX to a more flexible
and enriched soft phone installation using Microsoft Skype for Business.  This move saved
thousands of dollars by not replacing aging PBX equipment and put Navajo County in a leading
position in this technological landscape.  In addition, this year we have been required to purchase
additional Microsoft products for data classification by the Arizona State Auditors. 
 
Recommendation: 
Request the approval of the Microsoft Enterprise Agreement for the next three years.

Board of Supervisors Regular 9.a.        
Meeting Date: 04/11/2017  
Title: Broken Culvert
Submitted By: Melissa Buckley, Clerk of the Board of Supervisors
Department: Wht Mtn Lakes County Recreation Impr
Motion before the Board:
Consideration and approval to hire Phil Stratton Electric and Perkins Cinders to fix a broken
culvert and electrical repair at 2015 Silver Creek Drive. 
Presented By: Melissa Buckley
Background:
A broken culvert and electrical issue was discovered recently that could impact the safety of lake
goers and the community of White Mountain Lakes. White Mountain Lake Administrator met
with the WML Road District and Navajo County Public Works regarding the repair and both
indicated that they could not complete work on the subject matter because of private property.
The Administrator then contacted 5 electric companies and 8 excavating companies for quotes.
We have received 5 quotes and at this time would recommend the work be done by Phil Stratton
Electric and Perkins Cinders. 
Fiscal Impact
Fiscal Year: 16/17
Budgeted Y/N: Y
Amount Requested: $5343.00
Fiscal Impact:
This about will be taken out of the Grounds R&M 430.70
